No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. He always wore these tiny little glasses that sat right on the very end of his nose.\n\nDoctor: Since you were a child, have you usually had a hard time understanding other people and making friends?\n\nPatient: Making friends? No, I don't think it was hard. I had friends when I was little, I understood them fine. Like my friend Tyler in elementary school. We used to trade those little plastic alien figures at recess. They came in these round plastic capsules from the vending machines at the grocery store. You had to put in two quarters and turn the heavy metal knob, and it made this loud clacking sound. Clack, clack. The grocery store always smelled like bleach and old cardboard near the front doors. My mom used to get so many cardboard boxes from them when we moved. We packed everything in them, even the good plates. She wrapped the plates in newspaper so they wouldn't break, and the newspaper ink got all over my hands. It was black, but when you washed it in the sink it turned kind of grayish-purple. Purple is a weird color. You don't see a lot of purple cars on the highway.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you stay in odd postures for a long time, like holding your arms in the air without moving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess... I guess that's why they brought me in. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift. Or I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, or I'll do the hand signals, or I just move the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... keep doing it. Yelling is so loud in that warehouse because of the high ceilings. They have these huge metal rafters up there, and sometimes pigeons get inside and sit on them. Pigeons always bob their heads when they walk, it's so funny. I tried to feed one a piece of a hotdog bun once when I was sitting on a park bench. The bench had all this chipped green paint on it. Green paint always flakes off in these little curly pieces. My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.

He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I guess that's why they brought me in. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift. Or I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, or I'll do the hand signals, or I just move the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... keep doing it. Yelling is so loud in that warehouse because of the high ceilings. They have these huge metal rafters up there, and sometimes pigeons get inside and sit on them. Pigeons always bob their heads when they walk, it's so funny. I tried to feed one a piece of a hotdog bun once when I was sitting on a park bench. The bench had all this chipped green paint on it. Green paint always flakes off in these little curly pieces. My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\nDoctor: If I gently place your arm in a new position, does it tend to stay there on its own for a while?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know, I mean, you haven't tried to do that. I don't really think my arm would just stay there, but I guess I don't know for sure. I don't really like people touching my arms though. I got this really bad sunburn on my arms once when I fell asleep at the beach. The sand gets everywhere when you lay down on a towel. It's so hard to get sand out of your car floor mats once it's in there. I tried using those big vacuums at the car wash, the ones that take quarters? But they never have enough suction. Quarters are so heavy if you have a lot of them in your pocket. My grandpa used to collect state quarters in this big blue binder. He had almost all of them before he lost the binder when he moved to a new apartment. Moving is such a hassle, you have to pack everything into those cardboard boxes. Cardboard gives you the worst paper cuts if you're not careful. I got a paper cut on my thumb yesterday and it stung every time I washed my hands. The soap in the warehouse bathroom is that cheap pink stuff that smells like fake cherries. Cherries always have pits in them, which is annoying when you're trying to eat them. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess... they brought me in because of work. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like... well, freezing with my arms up by the forklift. It's like I have to hold the air. And I copy the beeping sounds, you know? Beep, beep, beep... and the hand signals the drivers make. I just do them over and over. And I move the boxes in these strange little patterns, even when the foreman and everyone is yelling for me to stop. They just yell so much. My cousin used to yell like that when we played video games. We played this one game with cars for hours, and he'd just scream if I crashed. I liked the blue car best, but the controller was sticky. It always smelled like orange soda. I don't drink soda anymore, though. The bubbles feel too loud.\n\nDoctor: When you do those movements or copy the beeps, is it because you feel you must do them to stop something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Stop something bad? No... no, I don't think so. It's not like that. I just... I just do it. Bad things just happen anyway, you know? Like when the forklift broke down last month. The mechanic came in, and he had this huge wrench. It was so shiny, it reminded me of the good silverware at my grandma's house. She used to polish it every Sunday before we ate. We always had roast beef on Sundays. I actually tried making roast beef once when I was living in the dorms, back when I was taking some classes. But my hot plate smoked up the whole room. The fire alarm went off, and it was just... so loud. Everyone had to stand outside in the parking lot in the snow. I didn't even have my boots on. My toes were freezing.\n\nDoctor: Right before you copy the beeps or make those movements, do you feel a buildup inside, like an urge or pressure that gets better once you do it?\n\nPatient: A buildup? Um, no... I don't feel any pressure or anything like that before I do it. It's not really an urge. It just happens. Pressure... the only pressure is the air compressor at the warehouse. They use it to fill the forklift tires, and it has this huge gauge on it with a little red needle. Red needles always remind me of compasses. My dad gave me a compass when we went camping upstate once. We slept in this green tent, but it leaked when it rained. It rained so much that weekend. The mud got absolutely everywhere, all over my sneakers. They were those white ones with the blue stripe. I tried to wash them in the sink when we got back, but the mud just stained the porcelain. The landlord was so mad about the sink. He always wore these tiny little glasses that sat right on the very end of his nose.\n\nDoctor: Since you were a child, have you usually had a hard time understanding other people and making friends?\n\nPatient: Making friends? No, I don't think it was hard. I had friends when I was little, I understood them fine. Like my friend Tyler in elementary school. We used to trade those little plastic alien figures at recess. They came in these round plastic capsules from the vending machines at the grocery store. You had to put in two quarters and turn the heavy metal knob, and it made this loud clacking sound. Clack, clack. The grocery store always smelled like bleach and old cardboard near the front doors. My mom used to get so many cardboard boxes from them when we moved. We packed everything in them, even the good plates. She wrapped the plates in newspaper so they wouldn't break, and the newspaper ink got all over my hands. It was black, but when you washed it in the sink it turned kind of grayish-purple. Purple is a weird color. You don't see a lot of purple cars on the highway.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you stay in odd postures for a long time, like holding your arms in the air without moving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess... I guess that's why they brought me in. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift. Or I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, or I'll do the hand signals, or I just move the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... keep doing it. Yelling is so loud in that warehouse because of the high ceilings. They have these huge metal rafters up there, and sometimes pigeons get inside and sit on them. Pigeons always bob their heads when they walk, it's so funny. I tried to feed one a piece of a hotdog bun once when I was sitting on a park bench. The bench had all this chipped green paint on it. Green paint always flakes off in these little curly pieces. My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\nDoctor: If I gently place your arm in a new position, does it tend to stay there on its own for a while?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know, I mean, you haven't tried to do that. I don't really think my arm would just stay there, but I guess I don't know for sure. I don't really like people touching my arms though. I got this really bad sunburn on my arms once when I fell asleep at the beach. The sand gets everywhere when you lay down on a towel. It's so hard to get sand out of your car floor mats once it's in there. I tried using those big vacuums at the car wash, the ones that take quarters? But they never have enough suction. Quarters are so heavy if you have a lot of them in your pocket. My grandpa used to collect state quarters in this big blue binder. He had almost all of them before he lost the binder when he moved to a new apartment. Moving is such a hassle, you have to pack everything into those cardboard boxes. Cardboard gives you the worst paper cuts if you're not careful. I got a paper cut on my thumb yesterday and it stung every time I washed my hands. The soap in the warehouse bathroom is that cheap pink stuff that smells like fake cherries. Cherries always have pits in them, which is annoying when you're trying to eat them. You have to spit the pits out into a napkin or something. Napkins at fast food places are always so thin, you need like ten of them just to wipe your hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices telling you to do these movements or copy the sounds?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't hear any voices telling me to do stuff like that. I mean, it's really loud at the warehouse anyway, so it's hard to hear people even when they're standing right next to you. The forklifts are always driving around, and they have that really high-pitched backup alarm. Beep, beep, beep. It echoes all the way up to the ceilings. The ceilings in there are just exposed corrugated metal. Metal gets so incredibly hot in the summer when the sun beats down on the roof. You could probably fry an egg on the hood of a car in that parking lot in July. I tried frying an egg on the sidewalk when I was a kid because I saw it on a cartoon, but it just made a gross, sticky mess. Eggs are so weird if you really think about it, just liquid inside a fragile little shell. I dropped a whole carton of eggs at the grocery store once and felt so bad. The floor was covered in this yellow slime. Yellow is a really intense color, like those high-visibility vests they make us wear at work. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, people say that a lot, actually. Like at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain the load sheet, but then my words just kind of jump... like, I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then suddenly I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then these boxes from church. And by the time I'm done talking, nobody has any idea what I meant. Church always smells like old wood and those little wax candles. They have those tiny little cups for communion, they look like little shot glasses but they're made of flimsy plastic. Plastic is so bad for the ocean, you know? I saw this documentary where a sea turtle had a plastic straw stuck in its nose, and the guys on the boat had to pull it out with pliers. Pliers are really handy to have around. I keep a pair in the glovebox of my car just in case. My glovebox is mostly full of old registration papers and ketchup packets from the drive-thru. Fast food ketchup always tastes sweeter than the kind you buy in the bottle at the store. Glass bottles are so heavy to carry in grocery bags, especially if the handles on the bags start stretching out.

I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\nDoctor: If I gently place your arm in a new position, does it tend to stay there on its own for a while?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know, I mean, you haven't tried to do that. I don't really think my arm would just stay there, but I guess I don't know for sure. I don't really like people touching my arms though. I got this really bad sunburn on my arms once when I fell asleep at the beach. The sand gets everywhere when you lay down on a towel. It's so hard to get sand out of your car floor mats once it's in there. I tried using those big vacuums at the car wash, the ones that take quarters? But they never have enough suction. Quarters are so heavy if you have a lot of them in your pocket. My grandpa used to collect state quarters in this big blue binder. He had almost all of them before he lost the binder when he moved to a new apartment. Moving is such a hassle, you have to pack everything into those cardboard boxes. Cardboard gives you the worst paper cuts if you're not careful. I got a paper cut on my thumb yesterday and it stung every time I washed my hands. The soap in the warehouse bathroom is that cheap pink stuff that smells like fake cherries. Cherries always have pits in them, which is annoying when you're trying to eat them. You have to spit the pits out into a napkin or something. Napkins at fast food places are always so thin, you need like ten of them just to wipe your hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices telling you to do these movements or copy the sounds?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't hear any voices telling me to do stuff like that. I mean, it's really loud at the warehouse anyway, so it's hard to hear people even when they're standing right next to you. The forklifts are always driving around, and they have that really high-pitched backup alarm. Beep, beep, beep. It echoes all the way up to the ceilings. The ceilings in there are just exposed corrugated metal. Metal gets so incredibly hot in the summer when the sun beats down on the roof. You could probably fry an egg on the hood of a car in that parking lot in July. I tried frying an egg on the sidewalk when I was a kid because I saw it on a cartoon, but it just made a gross, sticky mess. Eggs are so weird if you really think about it, just liquid inside a fragile little shell. I dropped a whole carton of eggs at the grocery store once and felt so bad. The floor was covered in this yellow slime. Yellow is a really intense color, like those high-visibility vests they make us wear at work. The reflective tape on mine is starting to peel off at the edges. Tape never sticks right once it gets dusty.\n\nDoctor: Do people tell you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people say that a lot, actually. Like at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain the load sheet, but then my words just kind of jump... like, I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then suddenly I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then these boxes from church. And by the time I'm done talking, nobody has any idea what I meant. Church always smells like old wood and those little wax candles. They have those tiny little cups for communion, they look like little shot glasses but they're made of flimsy plastic. Plastic is so bad for the ocean, you know? I saw this documentary where a sea turtle had a plastic straw stuck in its nose, and the guys on the boat had to pull it out with pliers. Pliers are really handy to have around. I keep a pair in the glovebox of my car just in case. My glovebox is mostly full of old registration papers and ketchup packets from the drive-thru. Fast food ketchup always tastes sweeter than the kind you buy in the bottle at the store. Glass bottles are so heavy to carry in grocery bags, especially if the handles on the bags start stretching out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't think so... I mean, people don't usually say my beliefs are strange. Sometimes they just don't listen to me anyway. Like the other guys at the warehouse, they just talk about sports or whatever they watched on TV. I don't really watch TV much anymore, the screen gives me a headache if I stare at it too long. My TV is one of those older flat screens, the kind with the really thick plastic border around the edge. Dust always settles right on that bottom edge, no matter how much you wipe it. I tried using those microfiber cloths, but they just sort of push the dust around instead of picking it up. You have to wash those cloths separate from your regular clothes, or else they get all these little lint balls stuck to them. Lint traps in dryers are so satisfying to clean out, you just peel it off in one big fuzzy layer. But if you forget to clean it, it's a huge fire hazard. Firefighters have to wear all that heavy gear, even in the summer. It must be so exhausting carrying all that weight around. I used to carry my backpack full of textbooks in college, and my shoulders would ache by the end of the day. Textbooks are ridiculously expensive, they make you buy a new edition every year just because they changed a couple of pictures. Pictures never look as good printed on paper as they do on a screen anyway.

My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices telling you to do these movements or copy the sounds?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't hear any voices telling me to do stuff like that. I mean, it's really loud at the warehouse anyway, so it's hard to hear people even when they're standing right next to you. The forklifts are always driving around, and they have that really high-pitched backup alarm. Beep, beep, beep. It echoes all the way up to the ceilings. The ceilings in there are just exposed corrugated metal. Metal gets so incredibly hot in the summer when the sun beats down on the roof. You could probably fry an egg on the hood of a car in that parking lot in July. I tried frying an egg on the sidewalk when I was a kid because I saw it on a cartoon, but it just made a gross, sticky mess. Eggs are so weird if you really think about it, just liquid inside a fragile little shell. I dropped a whole carton of eggs at the grocery store once and felt so bad. The floor was covered in this yellow slime. Yellow is a really intense color, like those high-visibility vests they make us wear at work. The reflective tape on mine is starting to peel off at the edges. And by the time I'm done talking, nobody has any idea what I meant. Church always smells like old wood and those little wax candles. They have those tiny little cups for communion, they look like little shot glasses but they're made of flimsy plastic. Plastic is so bad for the ocean, you know? I saw this documentary where a sea turtle had a plastic straw stuck in its nose, and the guys on the boat had to pull it out with pliers. Pliers are really handy to have around. I keep a pair in the glovebox of my car just in case. My glovebox is mostly full of old registration papers and ketchup packets from the drive-thru. Fast food ketchup always tastes sweeter than the kind you buy in the bottle at the store. Glass bottles are so heavy to carry in grocery bags, especially if the handles on the bags start stretching out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have beliefs that other people say are strange or not true?\n\nPatient: I don't think so... I mean, people don't usually say my beliefs are strange. Sometimes they just don't listen to me anyway. Like the other guys at the warehouse, they just talk about sports or whatever they watched on TV. I don't really watch TV much anymore, the screen gives me a headache if I stare at it too long. My TV is one of those older flat screens, the kind with the really thick plastic border around the edge. Dust always settles right on that bottom edge, no matter how much you wipe it. I tried using those microfiber cloths, but they just sort of push the dust around instead of picking it up. You have to wash those cloths separate from your regular clothes, or else they get all these little lint balls stuck to them. Lint traps in dryers are so satisfying to clean out, you just peel it off in one big fuzzy layer. But if you forget to clean it, it's a huge fire hazard. Firefighters have to wear all that heavy gear, even in the summer. It must be so exhausting carrying all that weight around. I used to carry my backpack full of textbooks in college, and my shoulders would ache by the end of the day. Textbooks are ridiculously expensive, they make you buy a new edition every year just because they changed a couple of pictures. Pictures never look as good printed on paper as they do on a screen anyway.\n\n[Last Question]\nFor at least one month, have these movement problems and hard-to-follow speech been happening most days?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Yeah, it's been going on for a while. The confusing talking and, you know, the weird stuff I do at the warehouse... it's been happening for about six months now. Six months is a long time, half a year. That's how long it takes to get a car's oil changed, if you drive a lot. Mayo goes bad really fast if you leave it out of the fridge. Refrigerators hum so loud at night when the house is quiet. It's like this low buzzing noise that just drills into your head. I saw a dog footprint in the sidewalk once, just this perfect little paw shape right in the middle of the square. Dogs are always sniffing everything, they have such a strong sense of smell. I wonder if they can smell the pollen before it even falls from the trees. Trees look so bare in the winter without their leaves.

We always had roast beef on Sundays. I actually tried making roast beef once when I was living in the dorms, back when I was taking some classes. But my hot plate smoked up the whole room. The fire alarm went off, and it was just... so loud. Everyone had to stand outside in the parking lot in the snow. I didn't even have my boots on. My toes were freezing.\n\nDoctor: Right before you copy the beeps or make those movements, do you feel a buildup inside, like an urge or pressure that gets better once you do it?\n\nPatient: A buildup? Um, no... I don't feel any pressure or anything like that before I do it. It's not really an urge. It just happens. Pressure... the only pressure is the air compressor at the warehouse. They use it to fill the forklift tires, and it has this huge gauge on it with a little red needle. Red needles always remind me of compasses. My dad gave me a compass when we went camping upstate once. We slept in this green tent, but it leaked when it rained. It rained so much that weekend. The mud got absolutely everywhere, all over my sneakers. They were those white ones with the blue stripe. I tried to wash them in the sink when we got back, but the mud just stained the porcelain. The landlord was so mad about the sink. He always wore these tiny little glasses that sat right on the very end of his nose.\n\nDoctor: Since you were a child, have you usually had a hard time understanding other people and making friends?\n\nPatient: Making friends? No, I don't think it was hard. I had friends when I was little, I understood them fine. Like my friend Tyler in elementary school. We used to trade those little plastic alien figures at recess. They came in these round plastic capsules from the vending machines at the grocery store. You had to put in two quarters and turn the heavy metal knob, and it made this loud clacking sound. Clack, clack. The grocery store always smelled like bleach and old cardboard near the front doors. My mom used to get so many cardboard boxes from them when we moved. We packed everything in them, even the good plates. She wrapped the plates in newspaper so they wouldn't break, and the newspaper ink got all over my hands. It was black, but when you washed it in the sink it turned kind of grayish-purple. Purple is a weird color. You don't see a lot of purple cars on the highway.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you stay in odd postures for a long time, like holding your arms in the air without moving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess... I guess that's why they brought me in. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift. Or I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, or I'll do the hand signals, or I just move the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... keep doing it. Yelling is so loud in that warehouse because of the high ceilings. They have these huge metal rafters up there, and sometimes pigeons get inside and sit on them. Pigeons always bob their heads when they walk, it's so funny. I tried to feed one a piece of a hotdog bun once when I was sitting on a park bench. The bench had all this chipped green paint on it. Green paint always flakes off in these little curly pieces. My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\nDoctor: If I gently place your arm in a new position, does it tend to stay there on its own for a while?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know, I mean, you haven't tried to do that. I don't really think my arm would just stay there, but I guess I don't know for sure. I don't really like people touching my arms though. I got this really bad sunburn on my arms once when I fell asleep at the beach. The sand gets everywhere when you lay down on a towel. It's so hard to get sand out of your car floor mats once it's in there. I tried using those big vacuums at the car wash, the ones that take quarters? But they never have enough suction. Quarters are so heavy if you have a lot of them in your pocket. My grandpa used to collect state quarters in this big blue binder. He had almost all of them before he lost the binder when he moved to a new apartment. Moving is such a hassle, you have to pack everything into those cardboard boxes. Cardboard gives you the worst paper cuts if you're not careful. I got a paper cut on my thumb yesterday and it stung every time I washed my hands. The soap in the warehouse bathroom is that cheap pink stuff that smells like fake cherries. Cherries always have pits in them, which is annoying when you're trying to eat them. You have to spit the pits out into a napkin or something. Napkins at fast food places are always so thin, you need like ten of them just to wipe your hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices telling you to do these movements or copy the sounds?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't hear any voices telling me to do stuff like that. I mean, it's really loud at the warehouse anyway, so it's hard to hear people even when they're standing right next to you. The forklifts are always driving around, and they have that really high-pitched backup alarm. Beep, beep, beep. It echoes all the way up to the ceilings. The ceilings in there are just exposed corrugated metal. Metal gets so incredibly hot in the summer when the sun beats down on the roof. You could probably fry an egg on the hood of a car in that parking lot in July. I tried frying an egg on the sidewalk when I was a kid because I saw it on a cartoon, but it just made a gross, sticky mess. Eggs are so weird if you really think about it, just liquid inside a fragile little shell. I dropped a whole carton of eggs at the grocery store once and felt so bad. The floor was covered in this yellow slime. Yellow is a really intense color, like those high-visibility vests they make us wear at work. The reflective tape on mine is starting to peel off at the edges. Tape never sticks right once it gets dusty.\n\nDoctor: Do people tell you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people say that a lot, actually. Like at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain the load sheet, but then my words just kind of jump... like, I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then suddenly I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then these boxes from church. And by the time I'm done talking, nobody has any idea what I meant. Church always smells like old wood and those little wax candles. They have those tiny little cups for communion, they look like little shot glasses but they're made of flimsy plastic. Plastic is so bad for the ocean, you know? I saw this documentary where a sea turtle had a plastic straw stuck in its nose, and the guys on the boat had to pull it out with pliers. Pliers are really handy to have around. I keep a pair in the glovebox of my car just in case. My glovebox is mostly full of old registration papers and ketchup packets from the drive-thru. Fast food ketchup always tastes sweeter than the kind you buy in the bottle at the store. Glass bottles are so heavy to carry in grocery bags, especially if the handles on the bags start stretching out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have beliefs that other people say are strange or not true?\n\nPatient: I don't think so... I mean, people don't usually say my beliefs are strange. Sometimes they just don't listen to me anyway. Like the other guys at the warehouse, they just talk about sports or whatever they watched on TV. I don't really watch TV much anymore, the screen gives me a headache if I stare at it too long. My TV is one of those older flat screens, the kind with the really thick plastic border around the edge. Dust always settles right on that bottom edge, no matter how much you wipe it. I tried using those microfiber cloths, but they just sort of push the dust around instead of picking it up. You have to wash those cloths separate from your regular clothes, or else they get all these little lint balls stuck to them. Lint traps in dryers are so satisfying to clean out, you just peel it off in one big fuzzy layer. But if you forget to clean it, it's a huge fire hazard. Firefighters have to wear all that heavy gear, even in the summer. It must be so exhausting carrying all that weight around. I used to carry my backpack full of textbooks in college, and my shoulders would ache by the end of the day. Textbooks are ridiculously expensive, they make you buy a new edition every year just because they changed a couple of pictures. Pictures never look as good printed on paper as they do on a screen anyway.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have these movement problems and hard-to-follow speech been happening most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been going on for a while. The confusing talking and, you know, the weird stuff I do at the warehouse... it's been happening for about six months now. Six months is a long time, half a year. That's how long it takes to get a car's oil changed, if you drive a lot. I always forget to change my oil until the little sticker on the windshield gets all faded and curled at the edges. Windshields get so dirty in the spring with all the pollen. The yellow dust just coats everything, it looks like someone dumped mustard powder all over the cars. Mustard is okay, but I prefer mayo on sandwiches. Mayo goes bad really fast if you leave it out of the fridge. Refrigerators hum so loud at night when the house is quiet. It's like this low buzzing noise that just drills into your head. Drills are super loud too, especially the heavy-duty ones they use for concrete. Concrete takes forever to dry, and if you step in it, you leave a footprint forever. I saw a dog footprint in the sidewalk once, just this perfect little paw shape right in the middle of the square. Dogs are always sniffing everything, they have such a strong sense of smell. I wonder if they can smell the pollen before it even falls from the trees. Trees look so bare in the winter without their leaves.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Yeah, it's... it's definitely been worse. They actually took me off forklift duties at the warehouse because they said they can't trust me to keep things safe around me anymore. And I've fallen way behind in my community college classes, too. I even stopped showing up for my shifts at the church food bank because people kept saying they couldn't count on me. Canned food is really heavy when you have to carry a whole box of it. Some of those cans don't even have the pull-tabs on top, you have to use a manual can opener. Manual can openers always get rusty if you wash them and don't dry them right away. Rust is so hard to get off metal, you have to scrub it with steel wool or something. Steel wool feels so scratchy, it's like touching a Brillo pad. Brillo pads always have that weird blue soap dried inside them. Soap smells nice, but if you get it in your eyes, it burns so bad. I got shampoo in my eye once and it was red for the rest of the day. Red is a warning color, like stop signs and fire trucks. Fire trucks are huge, I don't know how they turn those tight corners on city streets without hitting the parked cars. Parked cars get so hot inside during the summer, it's like an oven when you first open the door.

The fire alarm went off, and it was just... so loud. Everyone had to stand outside in the parking lot in the snow. I didn't even have my boots on. My toes were freezing.\n\nDoctor: Right before you copy the beeps or make those movements, do you feel a buildup inside, like an urge or pressure that gets better once you do it?\n\nPatient: A buildup? Um, no... I don't feel any pressure or anything like that before I do it. It's not really an urge. It just happens. Pressure... the only pressure is the air compressor at the warehouse. They use it to fill the forklift tires, and it has this huge gauge on it with a little red needle. Red needles always remind me of compasses. My dad gave me a compass when we went camping upstate once. We slept in this green tent, but it leaked when it rained. It rained so much that weekend. The mud got absolutely everywhere, all over my sneakers. They were those white ones with the blue stripe. I tried to wash them in the sink when we got back, but the mud just stained the porcelain. The landlord was so mad about the sink. He always wore these tiny little glasses that sat right on the very end of his nose.\n\nDoctor: Since you were a child, have you usually had a hard time understanding other people and making friends?\n\nPatient: Making friends? No, I don't think it was hard. I had friends when I was little, I understood them fine. Like my friend Tyler in elementary school. We used to trade those little plastic alien figures at recess. They came in these round plastic capsules from the vending machines at the grocery store. You had to put in two quarters and turn the heavy metal knob, and it made this loud clacking sound. Clack, clack. The grocery store always smelled like bleach and old cardboard near the front doors. My mom used to get so many cardboard boxes from them when we moved. We packed everything in them, even the good plates. She wrapped the plates in newspaper so they wouldn't break, and the newspaper ink got all over my hands. It was black, but when you washed it in the sink it turned kind of grayish-purple. Purple is a weird color. You don't see a lot of purple cars on the highway.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that you stay in odd postures for a long time, like holding your arms in the air without moving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess... I guess that's why they brought me in. At the warehouse, I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift. Or I'll copy the beeping sounds it makes when it backs up, or I'll do the hand signals, or I just move the boxes in these strange little patterns. Even when everyone is yelling for me to stop, I just... keep doing it. Yelling is so loud in that warehouse because of the high ceilings. They have these huge metal rafters up there, and sometimes pigeons get inside and sit on them. Pigeons always bob their heads when they walk, it's so funny. I tried to feed one a piece of a hotdog bun once when I was sitting on a park bench. The bench had all this chipped green paint on it. Green paint always flakes off in these little curly pieces. My uncle had a green truck that did that. He drove it all the way to Florida once to see the ocean. The ocean is so salty, it makes your skin feel all tight when it dries.\n\nDoctor: When someone moves your arm or body into a position, do you tend to stay in that exact position for a while?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't think anyone really moves my arms around for me. People don't usually touch me like that. Except maybe my supervisor when he grabs my shoulder to get my attention at the loading dock. He always wears these really thick leather work gloves. Leather gets so stiff when it's cold out, you know? Like if you leave it in your car overnight. I left my jacket in my car once during a snowstorm and it was basically frozen solid the next morning. My car's heater is totally busted anyway, it just blows out this freezing air and all this gray dust from the vents. Dust is actually mostly dead skin cells, which is pretty gross when you really think about it. I saw this whole documentary about dust mites on the TV in the breakroom once. The volume on that TV doesn't work, so it's just silent video of microscopic bugs and stuff crawling around. Bugs always have way too many legs. Centipedes are the worst ones.\n\nDoctor: Have people said that your face often looks blank or that you show very little emotion?\n\nPatient: No, nobody really says that to me. I don't think my face looks blank, I mean, I smile and stuff. I see my face in the mirror every morning before work. The mirror in our bathroom has this weird smudge in the corner that won't come off, no matter how much glass cleaner I spray on it. Glass cleaner always smells so strongly of ammonia, you know? It smells exactly like the heavy-duty stuff we use at the warehouse to mop the breakroom floors. The mop bucket we have is bright yellow and one of the little plastic wheels is totally busted, so it just squeaks and drags across the linoleum. Squeak, scrape. Linoleum gets so dangerously slippery when it's wet. My roommate slipped on some wet leaves outside our apartment stairs last week and completely ruined his favorite shoes. They were these canvas sneakers with red laces. Red is the color of stop signs, but people in my neighborhood just roll right through them anyway. It's really dangerous to just ignore traffic signs like that.\n\nDoctor: If I gently place your arm in a new position, does it tend to stay there on its own for a while?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know, I mean, you haven't tried to do that. I don't really think my arm would just stay there, but I guess I don't know for sure. I don't really like people touching my arms though. I got this really bad sunburn on my arms once when I fell asleep at the beach. The sand gets everywhere when you lay down on a towel. It's so hard to get sand out of your car floor mats once it's in there. I tried using those big vacuums at the car wash, the ones that take quarters? But they never have enough suction. Quarters are so heavy if you have a lot of them in your pocket. My grandpa used to collect state quarters in this big blue binder. He had almost all of them before he lost the binder when he moved to a new apartment. Moving is such a hassle, you have to pack everything into those cardboard boxes. Cardboard gives you the worst paper cuts if you're not careful. I got a paper cut on my thumb yesterday and it stung every time I washed my hands. The soap in the warehouse bathroom is that cheap pink stuff that smells like fake cherries. Cherries always have pits in them, which is annoying when you're trying to eat them. You have to spit the pits out into a napkin or something. Napkins at fast food places are always so thin, you need like ten of them just to wipe your hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices telling you to do these movements or copy the sounds?\n\nPatient: No, um... I don't hear any voices telling me to do stuff like that. I mean, it's really loud at the warehouse anyway, so it's hard to hear people even when they're standing right next to you. The forklifts are always driving around, and they have that really high-pitched backup alarm. Beep, beep, beep. It echoes all the way up to the ceilings. The ceilings in there are just exposed corrugated metal. Metal gets so incredibly hot in the summer when the sun beats down on the roof. You could probably fry an egg on the hood of a car in that parking lot in July. I tried frying an egg on the sidewalk when I was a kid because I saw it on a cartoon, but it just made a gross, sticky mess. Eggs are so weird if you really think about it, just liquid inside a fragile little shell. I dropped a whole carton of eggs at the grocery store once and felt so bad. The floor was covered in this yellow slime. Yellow is a really intense color, like those high-visibility vests they make us wear at work. The reflective tape on mine is starting to peel off at the edges. Tape never sticks right once it gets dusty.\n\nDoctor: Do people tell you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people say that a lot, actually. Like at work, when the supervisor asks me which pallets are going on the truck, I start trying to explain the load sheet, but then my words just kind of jump... like, I'll start talking about the load sheet, and then suddenly I'm talking about my old house, and then fishing, and then these boxes from church. And by the time I'm done talking, nobody has any idea what I meant. Church always smells like old wood and those little wax candles. They have those tiny little cups for communion, they look like little shot glasses but they're made of flimsy plastic. Plastic is so bad for the ocean, you know? I saw this documentary where a sea turtle had a plastic straw stuck in its nose, and the guys on the boat had to pull it out with pliers. Pliers are really handy to have around. I keep a pair in the glovebox of my car just in case. My glovebox is mostly full of old registration papers and ketchup packets from the drive-thru. Fast food ketchup always tastes sweeter than the kind you buy in the bottle at the store. Glass bottles are so heavy to carry in grocery bags, especially if the handles on the bags start stretching out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have beliefs that other people say are strange or not true?\n\nPatient: I don't think so... I mean, people don't usually say my beliefs are strange. Sometimes they just don't listen to me anyway. Like the other guys at the warehouse, they just talk about sports or whatever they watched on TV. I don't really watch TV much anymore, the screen gives me a headache if I stare at it too long. My TV is one of those older flat screens, the kind with the really thick plastic border around the edge. Dust always settles right on that bottom edge, no matter how much you wipe it. I tried using those microfiber cloths, but they just sort of push the dust around instead of picking it up. You have to wash those cloths separate from your regular clothes, or else they get all these little lint balls stuck to them. Lint traps in dryers are so satisfying to clean out, you just peel it off in one big fuzzy layer. But if you forget to clean it, it's a huge fire hazard. Firefighters have to wear all that heavy gear, even in the summer. It must be so exhausting carrying all that weight around. I used to carry my backpack full of textbooks in college, and my shoulders would ache by the end of the day. Textbooks are ridiculously expensive, they make you buy a new edition every year just because they changed a couple of pictures. Pictures never look as good printed on paper as they do on a screen anyway.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have these movement problems and hard-to-follow speech been happening most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been going on for a while. The confusing talking and, you know, the weird stuff I do at the warehouse... it's been happening for about six months now. Six months is a long time, half a year. That's how long it takes to get a car's oil changed, if you drive a lot. I always forget to change my oil until the little sticker on the windshield gets all faded and curled at the edges. Windshields get so dirty in the spring with all the pollen. The yellow dust just coats everything, it looks like someone dumped mustard powder all over the cars. Mustard is okay, but I prefer mayo on sandwiches. Mayo goes bad really fast if you leave it out of the fridge. Refrigerators hum so loud at night when the house is quiet. It's like this low buzzing noise that just drills into your head. Drills are super loud too, especially the heavy-duty ones they use for concrete. Concrete takes forever to dry, and if you step in it, you leave a footprint forever. I saw a dog footprint in the sidewalk once, just this perfect little paw shape right in the middle of the square. Dogs are always sniffing everything, they have such a strong sense of smell. I wonder if they can smell the pollen before it even falls from the trees. Trees look so bare in the winter without their leaves.\n\nDoctor: Over the last six months, have you been doing worse at work than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's definitely been worse. They actually took me off forklift duties at the warehouse because they said they can't trust me to keep things safe around me anymore. And I've fallen way behind in my community college classes, too. I even stopped showing up for my shifts at the church food bank because people kept saying they couldn't count on me. Canned food is really heavy when you have to carry a whole box of it. Some of those cans don't even have the pull-tabs on top, you have to use a manual can opener. Manual can openers always get rusty if you wash them and don't dry them right away. Rust is so hard to get off metal, you have to scrub it with steel wool or something. Steel wool feels so scratchy, it's like touching a Brillo pad. Brillo pads always have that weird blue soap dried inside them. Soap smells nice, but if you get it in your eyes, it burns so bad. I got shampoo in my eye once and it was red for the rest of the day. Red is a warning color, like stop signs and fire trucks. Fire trucks are huge, I don't know how they turn those tight corners on city streets without hitting the parked cars. Parked cars get so hot inside during the summer, it's like an oven when you first open the door.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others. Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nI guess... they brought me in because of work. My cousin used to yell like that when we played video games. We played this one game with cars for hours, and he'd just scream if I crashed. I liked the blue car best, but the controller was sticky. It always smelled like orange soda. I don't drink soda anymore, though. The bubbles feel too loud.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings). It was so shiny, it reminded me of the good silverware at my grandma's house. She used to polish it every Sunday before we ate. We always had roast beef on Sundays. I actually tried making roast beef once when I was living in the dorms, back when I was taking some classes. But my hot plate smoked up the whole room.
